Locations refer to the handwritten sheet within the indicated scan. Roman numerals in scan 002 are chapter entries. For the opposing-arrow sketches upper and lower refer to the original page. A vertical bar inside a reading separates graphic alternatives.

| 002 | XIV | qualifier | `h` | A tall stem precedes the hyphen in each adjective. |

The text uses ordinary capitals, with double strokes recorded on D where visible. The possible script S in the crossed heading on scan 43 is treated separately from the S at the bottom of its triangle.

Scan numbers refer to the archive PDF. L counts approximate lines of the manuscript text. On scan 108 the columns are named in upright reading orientation. Entries give the LaTeX spelling or the literal text selected. Variants are retained where neighbouring examples do not settle the ink. In particular the raised flecks on H lack the long descender needed for j.

The derivator D is marked where its alphabet first becomes uncertain on each mathematical sheet. A plain H and a looped H occur side by side on 122. The diagnostic distinction there is the left loop. HOT, Hot and the H in Hom do not all have the same ductus. For short morphism shafts, heads genuinely visible in the image take precedence over the reading of a similar shaft elsewhere. On 126 the repeated initial in <code>i_y</code> has a short upright followed by a lower y. No index j was securely identified in this set. On the typescripts the different weights of the headings and names do not furnish a mathematical double-struck capital.

# Readings and notation · cote 157-1, scans 227–241

## Notation table

Locations give the manuscript area and the corresponding lines in the individual TeX file. Capitals in headings were examined separately. A vertical bar between readings gives alternatives in ink-first order. A low numeral is distinguished from a prime, from an exponent and from the slashes in slice notation. In prose an opening double quote is two backticks; the closing quote is two apostrophes. The same family letter is marked at its first occurrence on each sheet where its face remains ambiguous. Doubtful heads on individual arrows are recorded at their own positions.

## Notation table

Locations count manuscript lines approximately. An underline on a mathematical name is transcribed independently of its capitalisation. The same pen can give a complete arrowhead in one example and a blunt shaft beside it. The alternatives belong to the individual occurrence or to the indicated recurring glyph.

# Readings and doubts

## Notation table

Each L number refers to a line in the corresponding page fragment. The position alongside it locates the ink on the sheet. The TeX in this table is a reading of the indicated occurrence. Primes, retraced diagonals and arrowheads sometimes differ from one occurrence to the next.

| Scan | Line and visual anchor | LaTeX or reading | Features and alternatives |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 291 | L4, upper, opening map | `$I_{\tau}$, $I_{\tau'}$` | Barred capital I; the small footed subscript allows \tau or t. Prime belongs to the subscript. |
| 291 | L16, comparison triangles | `$\mathbb{\Delta}$ or $\Delta$; $\tau$ or $t$` | The extra left diagonal and the short foot of the type letter are independent marks. The bare triangle on scan 292 provides a comparison. |
| 291 | L16, upper and middle prose | `int., isom.` | Abbreviated before orientés and before à des, respectively. |
| 291 | L17, upper, two comparison intervals | `$\mathbb{\Delta}_i^+,\mathbb{\Delta}_i^-$` | The left diagonal is doubled; the narrowest triangles also allow plain \Delta. |
| 291 | L22, middle, sequence display | `$\Sigma_\tau$, $\mathbb{\Delta}_{l_1}^{\varepsilon_1}(\tau)$` | The sequence begins with angular sigma. The looped l and its small numeral are low on the triangle. The parenthetical tau follows at baseline. The high curled sign is epsilon. |
| 291 | L34, lower, conditions | `$f_i$, $f_{i+1}$; a), b)` | Descender of f crosses its horizontal stroke. Index is i; terminal index r. |

A high circle is the first reading wherever it competes with a small o. The unruled Ty on scan 325 names the monoid. The ruled forms on 323, 327, 328 and 333 have a longer stroke through the second letter.

The headings use the same hand as the prose. Their tall Roman, Greek and script candidates were checked in the headings themselves. The reverse-side job print on scan 2 uses monospaced capital letters. The quotations in the manuscript use paired raised strokes. Single apostrophes mark elisions.

# Readings and doubts · cote 157-3, scans 066–081

## Notation table

The loci are approximate working line numbers on the individual sheet. They exclude the manuscript page number and archive foliation. A diagram, a display or an oblique note can count as one line. A candidate in parentheses is an alternative shape allowed by the ink. The first marked occurrence of a recurring shape governs later comparable occurrences on the same sheet; an independently damaged instance can have its own mark.

Across the sheets, flat horizontal strokes and headed arrows are distinguished where the ink allows it. A short rise at the end can belong to either. The coefficient written ξ has ζ as a recurring alternative. Only the cancelled form on 098 also admits g. The tall curled T on 111–113 and the crossed script X on 112–113 carry font-family doubts at their first occurrence on each sheet. The two parallel strokes equivalent to “même” in running text are recorded as = where they are clearly separate. On 110–112 the hooked strokes in the inclusion diagrams have distinct shapes from the two-headed implication signs on 105.
